We proposed a DML approach for estimating natural direct and indirect quantile treatment effects under a sequential ignorability assumption. The method relies on the efficient score functions of the potential outcomes’ cumulative distributional functions, which are inverted to compute the quantiles as well as the treatment effects (as the differences in potential outcomes at those quantiles). The robustness property of the efficient score functions permits estimating the nuisance parameters (outcome, treatment, and mediator models) by machine learning and cross-fitting avoids overfitting bias. We demonstrated that our quantile treatment effect estimators are root-n-consistent and asymptotically normal. Furthermore, we suggested a multiplier bootstrap and demonstrated its consistency for uniform statistical inference. We also investigated the finite sample performance of our estimators by means of a simulation study. Finally, we applied our method to data from the National Job Corp Study to evaluate the direct earnings effects of training across the earnings distribution, as well as the indirect effects operating via general health. We found positive and statistically significant direct effects across a large range of the earnings quantiles, while the indirect effects were generally close to zero and mostly statistically insignificant.

Appendix G
Appendix

G.1
Proof of Proposition 1

Proof.
The proof relies on using Assumptions 1.1 through 1.4. Under these assumptions, it can be shown that:

\displaystyle F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int P\left(Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)\leq a|X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx\text{ (by iterated expectation) }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|M\left(d^{\prime}\right)=m,X=x\right)dP\left(M\left(d^{\prime}\right)=m|X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by iterated expectation)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|D=d^{\prime},M\left(d^{\prime}\right)=m,X=x\right)

\displaystyle\times dP\left(M\left(d^{\prime}\right)=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx\text{ (by Assumption 2) }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|D=d^{\prime},M=m,X=x\right)dP\left(M=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by Assumption 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)dP\left(M=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by Assumption 3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|D=d,X=x\right)dP\left(M=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by Assumption 2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int P\left(Y\left(d,m\right)\leq a|D=d,M=m,X=x\right)dP\left(M=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by Assumption 3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int P\left(Y\leq a|D=d,M=m,X=x\right)dP\left(M=m|D=d^{\prime},X=x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dx

(by Assumption 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,m,x\right)f_{M|D,X}\left(m|d^{\prime},x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dmdx.

G.2
Derivations of the EIF

The derivation of the efficient influence function (EIF) of an estimand is based on calculating Gateaux derivatives for the estimand. Let
P
denote the true data generating distribution and
\Psi\left(P\right)
the estimand of interest, which is a statistical functional of
P
.
The Gateaux derivative of
\Psi\left(.\right)
measures how the estimand

\Psi\left(.\right)
changes as
P
shifts in the direction of another
distribution, say
\tilde{P}
. Let
P_{t}=t\tilde{P}+\left(1-t\right)P
,
where
t\in\left[0,1\right]
. Formally, the Gateaux derivative of
estimand
\Psi\left(.\right)
when changing
P
in the direction of

\tilde{P}
is defined as

\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\left(\frac{\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)-\Psi\left(P\right)}{t}\right)=\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0},

(G.1)

if the limit on the right-hand side exists. It can be shown that under
certain regularity conditions, the EIF of
\Psi\left(P\right)
under
the distribution
\tilde{P}
is equal to Gateaux derivative (
G.1
)

(
Hines et al. 2022
)
. This fact provides a convenient way of deriving
the EIF. Following
Hines et al. 2022
, we use the strategy of “point
mass contamination” to derive the EIF of
F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)
.
Specifically, we consider
\tilde{P}
to be a point mass of a single
observation, say
\tilde{o}
, and then the EIF of
\Psi\left(P\right)

evaluated at
\tilde{o}
is equal to the Gateaux derivative (
G.1
). This derivation strategy appears attractive when the treatment variable
D
is discrete.
3
3

3

Notice that if
D
is not discrete, this strategy can not be used,
and the derivation needs to rely on using other methods instead, see

Fisher and
Kennedy 2019
;
Levy 2019
;
Ichimura and
Newey 2022
.
Let

1_{\tilde{o}}\left(o\right)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }o=\tilde{o}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

denote the Dirac delta function with respect to
\tilde{o}
. If the
density function for
P
is
f_{O}\left(o\right)
, the density function
for
P_{t}
is
f_{O}^{t}\left(o\right)=t1_{\tilde{o}}\left(o\right)+\left(1-t\right)f_{O}\left(o\right)

and

\left.\frac{d}{dt}f_{O}^{t}\left(o\right)\right|_{t=0}=1_{\tilde{o}}\left(o\right)-f_{O}\left(o\right),

and
f_{O}^{t}\left(o\right)=f_{O}\left(o\right)
when
t=0
. Under Assumptions 1.1 to 1.4, it follows from
Proposition 1 that

\displaystyle F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,m,x\right)f_{M|D,X}\left(m|d^{\prime},x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right)dmdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int\int 1\left\{y\leq a\right\}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}f_{X}\left(x\right)dydmdx.

Let

\Psi\left(P_{t}\right):=\int\int\int 1\left\{y\leq a\right\}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)dydmdx.

We would like to calculate the Gateau derivative:

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0}=\left.\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int\int\int 1\left\{y\leq a\right\}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)dydmdx\right)\right|_{t=0}.

It can be shown that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int\int 1\left\{y\leq a\right\}\left.\left(\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}dydmdx

\displaystyle+\int\int\int 1\left\{y\leq a\right\}\left.\left(f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\right)\right|_{t=0}dydmdx.

Considering the expression within the integral of (
),

\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\times\left[1_{\tilde{x}}\left(x\right)-f_{X}\left(x\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{Y|D,M,X}\left(y|d,m.x\right)f_{M|D,X}\left(m|d^{\prime},x\right)1_{\tilde{x}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle-f_{Y|D,M,X}\left(y|d,m.x\right)f_{M|D,X}\left(m|d^{\prime},x\right)f_{X}\left(x\right).

Therefore, (
) can be further expressed as

E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,\tilde{x}\right)|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right]-E\left[g_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(X\right)\right].

(G.4)

Considering the xpression within the integral of (
),

\displaystyle\left.\left(f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\left(f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle+\left.\left(f_{X}^{t}\left(x\right)\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\right)\right|_{t=0}.

Considering (
),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{1}{f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle-\left.\left(\frac{f_{M,D,X}^{t}\left(m,d^{\prime},x\right)}{\left(f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)\right)^{2}}\frac{d}{dt}f_{D,X}^{t}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{f_{D,X}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)}\left[1_{\left(\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}\left(m,x\right)-f_{M|D,X}\left(m|d^{\prime},x\right)1_{\tilde{x}}\left(x\right)\right].

Using some algebra, the part of (
) appearing in (
)
can be expressed as

\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{f_{D|X}\left(d^{\prime}|\tilde{x}\right)}\left(F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)-E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,\tilde{x}\right)|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right]\right).

(G.7)

Concerning (
),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{1}{f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)}\frac{d}{dt}f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m.x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle-\left.\left(\frac{f_{Y,D,M,X}^{t}\left(y,d,m,x\right)}{\left(f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)\right)^{2}}\frac{d}{dt}f_{D,M,X}^{t}\left(d,m,x\right)\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{f_{D,M,X}\left(d,m,x\right)}\left[1_{\left(\tilde{y},\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}\left(y,m,x\right)1\left\{D=d\right\}-f_{Y,D,M,X}\left(y,d,m,x\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{Y|D,M,X}\left(y|d,m,x\right)}{f_{D,M,X}\left(d,m,x\right)}\left[1_{\left(\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}\left(m,x\right)1\left\{D=d\right\}-f_{D,M,X}\left(d,m,x\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{f_{D,M,X}\left(d,m,x\right)}\left[1_{\left(\tilde{y},\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}\left(y,m,x\right)-f_{Y|D,M,X}\left(y|d,m,x\right)1_{\tilde{m},\tilde{x}}\left(m,x\right)\right].

Furthermore, the part of (
) appearing in (
)
can be expressed as

\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{f_{D|X}\left(d|\tilde{x}\right)}\frac{f_{M|D,X}\left(\tilde{m}|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right)}{f_{M|D,X}\left(\tilde{m}|d,\tilde{x}\right)}\left(1\left\{\tilde{y}\leq a\right\}-F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)\right).

(G.8)

Combing (
G.4
), (
G.7
) and (
G.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,\tilde{x}\right)|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right]-E\left[g_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(X\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{f_{D|X}\left(d^{\prime}|\tilde{x}\right)}\left(F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)-E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,\tilde{x}\right)|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{f_{D|X}\left(d|\tilde{x}\right)}\frac{f_{M|D,X}\left(\tilde{m}|d^{\prime},\tilde{x}\right)}{f_{M|D,X}\left(\tilde{m}|d,\tilde{x}\right)}\left(1\left\{\tilde{y}\leq a\right\}-F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)\right).

If we replace the notation
\left(\tilde{y},\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)

with
\left(Y,M,X\right)
and notice that
g_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(X\right):=E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,X\right)|d^{\prime},X\right]

and
E\left[g_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(X\right)\right]=F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)
,
then
E\left[\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0}\right]=0

implies that

F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)=E\left[\psi^{\prime}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{\prime}\right)\right],

where
\psi^{\prime}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{\prime}\right)
is defined
in equation (
14
). We can apply the Bayes rule to rewrite
the term (
G.8
) as

\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{f_{D|X}\left(d^{\prime}|\tilde{x}\right)}\frac{f_{D|M,X}\left(d^{\prime}|\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}{f_{D|M,X}\left(d|\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)}\left(1\left\{\tilde{y}\leq a\right\}-F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,\tilde{m},\tilde{x}\right)\right),

and
E\left[\left.\frac{d}{dt}\Psi\left(P_{t}\right)\right|_{t=0}\right]=0

now implies that

F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d^{\prime}\right)\right)}\left(a\right)=E\left[\psi\left(W_{a},v_{a}\right)\right],

where
\psi\left(W_{a},v_{a}\right)
is defined in equation (
11
).

G.3
Proofs of Theorems in Section 3

Proof of Theorem 1.

The proof relies on Theorem A.1 in Appendix A.4, which states that K-fold cross-fitting is uniformly valid for estimating a parameter of interest under certain regularity conditions. We show that the conditions in Assumption 2 are sufficient for the proposed K-fold cross-fitting estimator to satisfy Assumptions A.1.1 to A.1.8, which are required for establishing Theorem A.1. Notice that the condition
\left\|\hat{v}_{a}-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}
in Assumption 2.4 already satisfies Assumption A.1.8. For this reason, we will only verify Assumption A.1.1 to A.1.7 in the subsequent discussion. We first derive several preliminary results which are useful for the proof to follow.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{an}
be the set of

v:=\left(g_{1d}\left(X\right),g_{2d}\left(M,X\right),g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right),g_{4ad}\left(D,X\right)\right).

g_{1d}\left(X\right),g_{2d}\left(M,X\right),g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right)

and
g_{4ad}\left(D,X\right)
are
P
-integrable functions such
that for
d\in\left\{0,1\right\}
,

\displaystyle P\left(\varepsilon_{1}<g_{1d}\left(X\right)<1-\varepsilon_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=1,

(G.9)

\displaystyle P\left(\varepsilon_{2}<g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)<1-\varepsilon_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=1,

(G.10)

where
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\left(0,1/2\right)
, and
with probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
, for
d\in\left\{0,1\right\}
,
all
a\in\mathcal{A}
and
q\geq 4
,

\displaystyle\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,q}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-0.5\right\|_{P,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 0.5-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-0.5\right\|_{P,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 0.5-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{4ad}\left(D,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Notice that
\hat{v}_{a}\in\mathcal{G}_{an}
by Assumption 2.4. Proving the result for all functions in

\mathcal{G}_{an}
implies that it also holds for
\hat{v}_{a}
. By
Assumption 2.2, it can be shown that for
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
,
the event

0<\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{1-\varepsilon_{2}}<\frac{g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{g_{2d^{\prime}}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}<\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{2}}<\infty

holds with probability one. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}

\displaystyle=\left(E\left[E\left[\left|g_{3a}\left(D,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right|^{q}|M,X\right]\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(E\left[\sum_{d\in\left\{0,1\right\}}\left|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right|^{q}g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{2}^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(E\left[\sum_{d\in\left\{0,1\right\}}\left|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{2}^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\max_{d\in\left\{0,1\right\}}E\left[\left|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

which implies that for
d\in\left\{0,1\right\}
and all
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
with
P
probability at least
1-\Delta_{n}
,

\left\|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\leq C\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}},

by the assumption
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,q}\leq C

and
\varepsilon_{2}>0
. A similar argument can be used to show that
for
d\in\left\{0,1\right\}
and all
a\in\mathcal{A}
, with
probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

(G.11)

\displaystyle\left\|g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-g_{4d}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

(G.12)

by assumption
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

and
\varepsilon_{2}>0
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(G.13)

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(G.14)

\displaystyle\left\|g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(G.15)

Verifying Assumption A.1.1:
F_{Y(d,M(d^{\prime}))}^{0}(a)=\theta_{d,d^{\prime},a}^{0}

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

Recall that

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a};v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\times\left[Y_{a}-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]

(G.16)

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\left[g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]+g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right).

(G.17)

Conditional on
M
and
X
, the expectation of the first term after the equals sign in expression (
G.16
)
is

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}Y_{a}|M,X\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\times E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}Y_{a}|M,X\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\times g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right),

and the expectation of the last term right in expression (
G.16
) is

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)|M,X\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\times g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right).

Therefore, conditional on
M
and
X
, the term in expression (
G.16
)
is zero and its unconditional expectation is also zero. Concerning the terms
in expression (
G.17
), notice that the first term

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\left[g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]|X\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}E\left[g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}|X\right]

\displaystyle-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)

\displaystyle=0

Therefore, the expectation of the first term in expression (
G.17
)
is zero. The expectation of the second term of in expression (
G.17
)
is
E\left[g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]=F_{Y(d,M(d^{\prime}))}^{0}(a)
by Proposition 1. Therefore, it follows that
F_{Y(d,M(d^{\prime}))}^{0}(a)=\theta_{d,d^{\prime},a}^{0}
.

The case when
d=d

Now, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a};v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\times\left[Y_{a}-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]+g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right),

(G.18)

where
g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)=F_{Y|D,X}^{0}\left(a|d,X\right)
. Concerning
the first term right of the equals sign in equation (
G.18
),

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\times\left[Y_{a}-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]|X\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}Y_{a}|X\right]}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Concerning the last term in equation (
G.18
), notice that

F_{Y|D,X}^{0}\left(a|d,X\right)=F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d\right)\right)|D,X}^{0}\left(a|d,X\right)=F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d\right)\right)|X}^{0}\left(a|X\right)

by Assumptions 1.1 and 1.2, and therefore,

E\left[g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]=E\left[F_{Y\left(d,M\left(d\right)\right)|X}^{0}\left(a|X\right)\right]=F_{Y(d,M(d))}^{0}(a).

Combining the previous results, it follows that
F_{Y(d,M(d^{\prime}))}^{0}(a)=\theta_{d,d^{\prime},a}^{0}

holds for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
and
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
.

Verifying Assumption A.1.2

If we treat
v
as deterministic, the second order Gateau derivative of the map
v\longmapsto E\left[\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a};v\right)\right]
exists and is continuous on
v\in\mathcal{G}_{an}
, and this property holds for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
. Therefore
v\longmapsto E\left[\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a};v\right)\right]
is twice continuously Gateau-differetiable for
a\in\mathcal{A}
.

Verifying Assumption A.1.3 (Neyman near orthogonality)

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

Recall that

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\left[1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\left[g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]+g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right).

Let

\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-t_{2}\right)}{\left(1-t_{1}\right)t_{2}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-t_{3}\right)+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-t_{1}}\left(t_{3}-t_{4}\right)+t_{4},

where
\mathbf{t}=\left(t_{1,},\ldots,t_{4}\right)
. If we set
\mathbf{t}=\left(g_{1d}\left(X\right),g_{2d}\left(M,X\right),g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right),g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right)
,
then
E\left[\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)\right]=E\left[\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)\right]
.
If we set

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}^{0}=\left(g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right),g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right),g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right),g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right),

then
E\left[\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\right]=E\left[\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right]
.
Furthermore,
\left\|\partial_{v}E\left[\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\left[v-v_{a}^{0}\right]\right]\right\|=\left\|E\left[\partial_{\mathbf{t}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{t}_{0}\right]\right]\right\|
.
The partial derivatives with respect to
\left(t_{1},\ldots,t_{4}\right)

are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{1}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-t_{2}\right)}{\left(1-t_{1}\right)^{2}t_{2}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-t_{3}\right)+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{(1-t_{1})^{2}}\left(t_{3}-t_{4}\right),

(G.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{2}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{\left(1-t_{1}\right)t_{2}^{2}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-t_{3}\right),

(G.20)

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{3}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-t_{1}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-t_{2}\right)}{\left(1-t_{1}\right)t_{2}},

(G.21)

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{4}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-t_{1}}.

(G.22)

Replacing
\mathbf{t}
with
\mathbf{t}^{0}
and taking expectations in equation (
G.19
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{1}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)^{2}g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\times\left[E\left[1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}1\left\{D=d\right\}|M,X\right]\right.\right.

(G.23)

\displaystyle\left.\left.-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]\left[g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)^{2}}\left[E\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)|X\right]\right.\right.

(G.24)

\displaystyle\left.-\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]\left[g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]

\displaystyle=0,

because in expressions (
G.23
) and (
G.24
), we have that

\displaystyle E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}|M,X\right]

\displaystyle=P_{Y,D|M,X}^{0}\left(Y\leq a,D=d|M,X\right)

\displaystyle=g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right),

(G.25)

\displaystyle E1\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)|X\right]

\displaystyle=\int g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,m,X\right)P_{D,M|X}^{0}\left(D=d^{\prime},M=m|X\right)dm

\displaystyle=\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right).

When taking expectations in equation (
G.20
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{2}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[-\frac{1}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)\left(g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right)^{2}}\right.

(G.26)

\displaystyle\times\left[E\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}|M,X\right]-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\times\left[g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=0,

by making use of expression (
G.25
). When taking expectations in equation (
G.21
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{3}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}|M,X\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left[g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=0,

because

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}|M,X\right]

\displaystyle=0.

When taking expectations in equation (
G.22
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{4}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[E\left[\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\right)|X\right]\left[g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=0,

since

E\left[\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\right)|X\right]=1-\frac{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}=0.

The case when
d=d^{\prime}

Recall that

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\left[1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]+g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right).

Now
\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)
is given by

\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{t_{1}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-t_{4}\right)+t_{4}.

The partial derivatives with respect to
\left(t_{1},t_{4}\right)

are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{1}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{t_{1}^{2}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-t_{4}\right),

(G.27)

\displaystyle\partial_{t_{4}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{t_{1}}.

(G.28)

Replacing
\mathbf{t}
with
\mathbf{t}^{0}
and taking expectation in equation (
G.27
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{1}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\frac{1}{\left(g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right)^{2}}\times\left[E\left[1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}1\left\{D=d\right\}|X\right]\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]\left[g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=0,

since the term

\displaystyle g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)

\displaystyle=P_{Y,D,M,X}^{0}\left(Y\leq a,d|X\right)=E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}Y_{a}|X\right].

Taking expectations in equation (
G.28
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\partial_{t_{4}}\mu_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(\mathbf{t}_{0}\right)\left[g_{4ad}\left(d,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[E\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}|X\right]\left[g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=0,

since

E\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}|X\right]=1-\frac{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}=0.

Combining all previous results, it follows that

\left\|\partial_{v}E\left[\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\left[v-v_{a}^{0}\right]\right]\right\|=0

holds for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}

and all
a\in\mathcal{A}
. Therefore,

\left\|\partial_{v}E\left[\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\left[v-v_{a}^{0}\right]\right]\right\|=0

holds all
a\in\mathcal{A}
.

Verifying Assumption A.1.4a
\left(r_{n}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right)

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

We have

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\right\}1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}\left(M,X\right)}\right\}\times g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)

\displaystyle-\left\{\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left[1-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right]}{\left[1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right]g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)}\right\}\times g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)

\displaystyle+\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}\left(X\right)}\right]g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)-\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\right]g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right).

To ease the notation, we express these nuisance parameters without their arguments in the following proof. Using the Minkowski inequality
yields

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{1}+\Pi_{2}+\Pi_{3},

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}\right\|_{P,2},

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}\right]\times g_{3a}-\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]\times g_{3a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2},

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}}\right)g_{4ad}-\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}\right)g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}.

In the following, Assumption 2.4 and the boundedness conditions (
G.9
),
(
G.10
), (
G.11
) and (
G.12
) are applied
to derive the relevant upper bounds. For the term
\Pi_{1}
, with
probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{g_{2d}^{0}-g_{2d}}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}\right\|_{P,2}+\left\|\frac{\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}-\frac{\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{2}^{0}-g_{2}\right\|_{P,2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{2}-g_{2}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\left\|g_{1}-g_{1}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\right)\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

,
by making use of the fact that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}-\frac{1}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{g_{2d}^{0}-g_{2d}}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}g_{2d}^{0}}+\frac{g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}}\left|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right|+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\left|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right|

and Assumption
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}
.
For the term
\Pi_{2}
, it is known that with probability
P
at
least
1-\Delta_{n}
, for
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\left(2g_{2d}-1\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}\times\left(g_{3d}-g_{3d}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}+\left\|\left[\frac{2g_{2d}-1}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}-\frac{2g_{2d}^{0}-1}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]\times g_{3a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}+\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}}\left\|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}+\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}+\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

by using

\frac{2g_{2d}-1}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}\leq\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2g_{2d}-1}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}}-\frac{2g_{2d}^{0}-1}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left[\frac{g_{2d}^{0}-g_{2d}}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)g_{2d}g_{2d}^{0}}+\frac{g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}}{\left(1-g_{1d}\right)\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]\times\left(2g_{2d}-1\right)+\frac{2\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}}\left|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right|+\frac{1-2\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\left|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right|+\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right|,

and Assumption
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

as well as condition (
G.11
). For the term
\Pi_{3}
, we can show
that with probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
and for
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{g_{1d}^{0}-g_{1d}}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left(1-g_{1d}\right)}g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}+\left\|\frac{g_{4ad}^{0}}{1-g_{1d}}-\frac{g_{4ad}}{1-g_{1d}}\right\|_{P,2}+\left\|g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\left\|g_{1d}^{0}-g_{1d}\right\|_{P,2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4ad}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\right]\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

by using
0\leq g_{4ad}^{0}\leq 1
, Assumption

\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

and condition (
G.12
).

The case when
d=d^{\prime}

We have

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\right\}1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}

\displaystyle+\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{g_{1d}}\right]g_{4ad}-\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right]g_{4ad}^{0},

where
g_{4ad}=g_{4ad}\left(d,X\right)
(not
g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)
).
Using the triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{4}+\Pi_{5},

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{4}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}\left(X\right)}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)}\right]1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}\right\|_{P,2},

\displaystyle\Pi_{5}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{g_{1d}}\right]g_{4ad}-\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right]g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}.

Following previous arguments, it can be shown that
\Pi_{4}\leq\varepsilon_{2}^{-2}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Similar as for
\Pi_{3}
, we have for
\Pi_{5}
,

\Pi_{5}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\left\|g_{1d}^{0}-g_{1d}\right\|_{P,2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4ad}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\left[\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\right]\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Combining the previous results, we obtain that with probability
P

at least
1-\Delta_{n}
and for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\left\|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

holds for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
. Therefore, with probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
and
all
a\in\mathcal{A}
and
v\in\mathcal{G}_{an}
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a},v\right)-\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Verifying Assumption A.1.4b
\left(\lambda_{n}^{\prime}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

We may write
\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)

as

\displaystyle\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left\{1-\left[r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0}\right]\right\}}{\left\{1-\left[r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0}\right]\right\}\left\{r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0}\right\}}

\displaystyle\times\left\{Y_{a}-\left[r\left(g_{3d}-g_{3d}^{0}\right)+g_{3d}^{0}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-\left[r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0}\right]}

\displaystyle\times\left\{r\left[\left(g_{3d}-g_{4ad}\right)-\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right]+\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right].

Let

\displaystyle A_{1}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=1-\left[r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0}\right],\text{ }A_{2}\left(r\right)=1-\left[r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0}\right],

\displaystyle A_{3}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0},\text{ }A_{4}\left(r\right)=Y_{a}-\left[r\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)+g_{3a}^{0}\right],

\displaystyle A_{5}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=r\left[\left(g_{3a}-g_{4ad}\right)-\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right]+\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right).

Notice that the functions
A_{i}\left(r\right)
,
i=1,\ldots,5

are also functions of the random variables
\left(Y_{a},M,X\right)
.
For this reason, we may rewrite
\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)

as

\displaystyle\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(r\right)}{A_{2}\left(r\right)A_{3}\left(r\right)}\times A_{4}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{A_{2}\left(r\right)}\times A_{5}\left(r\right)+\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right].

After some calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{2}E\left[\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)\right)\right]\right|_{r=\bar{r}}

\displaystyle=-E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\left(A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}}\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}\left(A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)\right]

(G.29)

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\left(A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\left(A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}}\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}A_{5}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}A_{5}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left[g_{3a}-g_{4ad}-\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right]\right].

To bound the expectation of the second order derivative above, we can
use the properties of
A_{i}\left(r\right)
. Using Assumption 2.2 and
acknowledging that
\bar{r}\in\left(0,1\right)
, we have that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}<A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\left[r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0}\right]<1-\varepsilon_{2},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}<A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\left[r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0}\right]<1-\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}<A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)

\displaystyle=r\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)+g_{2d}^{0}<1-\varepsilon_{1}

hold with probability one. Also
\left|A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right|

and
\left|A_{5}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right|
are bounded by constants, since
\left|Y_{a}\right|
,
g_{3a}
,
g_{3a}^{0}
,

g_{4ad}
and
g_{4ad}^{0}
are all bounded and
\bar{r}\in\left(0,1\right)
.
Based on these results and Assumption 2.4, it can be shown that the absolute
values of those terms on the right hand side of equation (
G.29
)
that involve interaction terms are all bounded by
\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}
.
We now consider the terms on the right hand side of equation (
G.29
)
that involve quadratic terms (the first, fourth, seventh and
ninth terms). By the assumption
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-1/4}
,
we have
\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-1/4}

and
\left\|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-1/4}
.
Concerning the first term,

\displaystyle\left|-E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|-E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(Y_{a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

(G.30)

\displaystyle+\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\bar{r}\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

since

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(Y_{a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\frac{\left(g_{1}-g_{1}^{0}\right)^{2}}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\times\left(P_{Y,D|M,X}^{0}\left(Y\leq a,D=d|M,X\right)-g_{2d}^{0}g_{3a}^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=0,

and

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\bar{r}\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{2}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\right|\bar{r}\left|g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right|\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{2}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

by assuming that
\left\|v-v_{a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}

and
\left|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right|<1-2\varepsilon_{1}
with probability
one. Applying a similar argument to the fourth term,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|E\left[\frac{A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\times E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(Y_{a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)|M,X\right]\right]\right|

\displaystyle+E\left[\left|\frac{A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)}\right|\bar{r}\left|g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right|\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 0+\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-\varepsilon_{2}\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{3}\varepsilon_{2}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

For the ninth term,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}A_{5}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|E\left[\frac{\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}E\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)|X\right]\right]\right|

\displaystyle+E\left[\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{\left(A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}\right|\bar{r}\left|\left(g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right)-\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right|\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{3}}\left(\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

since the term

\displaystyle E\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)|X\right]

\displaystyle=\int F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,m,X\right)f_{M|D|X}\left(m|d^{\prime}X\right)f_{D|X}\left(d^{\prime}|X\right)dm

\displaystyle-f_{D|X}\left(d^{\prime}|X\right)E\left[F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,X\right)|d^{\prime},X\right]

\displaystyle=0.

For the seventh term,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{1}\left(\bar{r}\right)A_{4}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{2}\left(\bar{r}\right)\left(A_{3}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right)^{3}}\left(g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 0+\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-\varepsilon_{2}\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon_{2}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{3}}\left\|g_{3a}-g_{3a}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-\varepsilon_{2}\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon_{2}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}^{3}}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

by
\left|g_{2d}-g_{2d}^{0}\right|<1-2\varepsilon_{2}
with probability
one.

The case when
d=d^{\prime}

Let

\displaystyle A_{6}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0},

\displaystyle A_{7}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=Y_{a}-\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right],

where
g_{4ad}=g_{4ad}\left(d,X\right)
. It holds that
\varepsilon_{1}<A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)<1-\varepsilon_{1}

and
\left|A_{7}\left(\bar{r}\right)\right|
for
\bar{r}\in\left[0,1\right]

are bounded, since
\left|Y_{a}\right|
,
g_{4ad}
and
g_{4ad}^{0}

are bounded. Then,

\displaystyle\psi_{{}_{d,d,a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{r\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)+g_{1d}^{0}}\left\{Y_{a}-\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right]\right\}+\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)}A_{7}\left(\bar{r}\right)+\left[r\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)+g_{4ad}^{0}\right].

After some calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{2}E\left[\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\psi_{{}_{d,d,a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)\right)\right]\right|_{r=\bar{r}}

\displaystyle=E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{7}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{3}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{2}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right].

Considering the first term on the right hand side, we have that

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}A_{7}\left(\bar{r}\right)}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{3}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|E\left[\frac{1}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{3}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)^{2}\left(E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}Y_{a}|X\right]-g_{1d}^{0}g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle+E\left[\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\bar{r}}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{3}}\right|\left|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right|\left|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right|\left|g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{r}\left(1-2\varepsilon_{1}\right)}{\varepsilon_{1}^{3}}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

since
E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}Y_{a}|X\right]-g_{1d}^{0}g_{4ad}^{0}=0

and

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|g_{1d}\left(X\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{4ad}\left(D,X\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Concerning the second term on the right hand side,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{A_{6}\left(\bar{r}\right)^{2}}\left(g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right)\left(g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\left\|g_{1d}-g_{1d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\left\|g_{4ad}-g_{4ad}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, if
v=v_{a}^{0}
, it is trivial to see that
\left.E\left[\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)\right)\right]\right|_{r=\bar{r}}=0

for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
,

a\in\mathcal{A}
and
\bar{r}\in\left(0,1\right)
. Combining the previous results, it follows that with probability
P
at least

1-\Delta_{n}
, for
\bar{r}\in\left(0,1\right)
, all
a\in\mathcal{A}

and
v\in\mathcal{G}_{an}\cup v_{a}^{0}
, we have that

\left|\left.E\left[\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\psi_{{}_{d,d^{\prime},a}}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)\right)\right]\right|_{r=\bar{r}}\right|\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

and this result holds for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
.
Therefore, with probability
P
at least
1-\Delta_{n}
,

\left\|\left.E\left[\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a};r\left(v-v_{a}^{0}\right)+v_{a}^{0}\right)\right)\right]\right|_{r=\bar{r}}\right\|\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}

holds for
\bar{r}\in\left(0,1\right)
, all
a\in\mathcal{A}
and

v\in\mathcal{G}_{an}\cup v_{a}^{0}
.

Verifying Assumption A.1.5 (Smoothness condition)

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

We may write
\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)

as

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\left(Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{3\bar{a}}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1}^{0}\left(d,X\right)}\right)\left(g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right).

Using the Minkowski inequality yields

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{1}\left(a\right)+\Pi_{2}\left(a\right)+\Pi_{3}\left(a\right),

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\left(Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right)\right\|_{P,2},

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right]\left(g_{3a}^{0}-g_{3\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2},

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1}^{0}\left(d,X\right)}\right)\left(g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}.

For
\Pi_{1}\left(a\right)
, we note that

\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|\leq\left|\frac{1-g_{2d}^{0}}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}

with probability one, which implies that

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}.

For
\Pi_{2}\left(a\right)
, we note that

\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2d}^{0}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}

with probability one, which implies that

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\left\|g_{3a}^{0}-g_{3\bar{a}}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2},

because

\displaystyle\left\|g_{3a}^{0}-g_{3\bar{a}}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(E\left[1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}.

For
\Pi_{3}\left(a\right)
, we note that

\left|1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}\right|\leq\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}\left(a\right)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}\leq\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2},

because

\displaystyle\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(E\left[1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}\left(E\left[Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}|d,M,X\right]\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}.

Combining the previous results, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}.

By Assumption 2.1, for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
,
we then obtain

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d^{\prime},\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}=0

as
d_{\mathcal{A}}\left(a,\bar{a}\right)\rightarrow 0
.

The case when
d=d^{\prime}

\displaystyle\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d,\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-1\left\{Y\leq\bar{a}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right]\left(g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right),

where
g_{4ad}^{0}=g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)
. By the triangle
inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d,\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{4}\left(a\right)+\Pi_{5}\left(a\right)\lesssim\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2},

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{4}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\left(1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}-1\left\{Y\leq\bar{a}\right\}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}

\displaystyle\Pi_{5}\left(a\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[1-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right]\left(g_{4ad}^{0}-g_{4\bar{a}d}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\leq\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}

By Assumption 2.1, for each
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
,
we obtain

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\psi_{d,d,\bar{a}}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|Y_{a}-Y_{\bar{a}}\right\|_{P,2}=0

as
d_{\mathcal{A}}\left(a,\bar{a}\right)\rightarrow 0
.

Combining the previous results, it follows

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)-\boldsymbol{\psi}_{a}\left(W_{\bar{a}},v_{\bar{a}}^{0}\right)\right\|_{P,2}=0

as
d_{\mathcal{A}}\left(a,\bar{a}\right)\rightarrow 0
.

Verifying Assumption A.1.6

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

Let
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{0}=\left\{g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right):d\in\left\{0,1\right\}\right\}

\mathcal{G}_{2}^{0}=\left\{g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right):d\in\left\{0,1\right\}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}^{0}

\displaystyle=\left\{g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right):a\in\mathcal{A},d\in\left\{0,1\right\}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}

\displaystyle=\left\{g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right):a\in\mathcal{A},\left\{d,d^{\prime}\right\}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}\right\},

\mathcal{G}_{5}=\left\{Y_{a}:a\in\mathcal{A}\right\}
,
\mathcal{G}_{6}=\left\{1\left\{D=d\right\}:d\in\left\{0,1\right\}\right\}
.
The union
\cup_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{0}
forms the set
\mathcal{G}_{a}

as defined above. By our assumptions,
g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)
and
g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)

are bounded within the interval
\left(0,1\right)
with probability
one.
g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)=F_{Y|D,M,X}\left(a|d,M,X\right)

is a conditional c.d.f. and
g_{4}^{0}\left(a,d,d^{\prime}X\right)=E\left[g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)|d^{\prime},X\right]

is a conditional expectation of a c.d.f., which are also
bounded for
a\in\mathcal{A}
with probability one. The functions

Y_{a}=1\left\{Y\leq a\right\}
and
1\left\{D=d\right\}
are
indicator functions and are bounded with probability one. In conclusion,
functions in the sets
\mathcal{G}_{j}^{0}
,
j=1,\ldots,4
are
uniformly bounded and their envelop functions are all bounded by some
constant. By Assumption 1 and Lemma L.2 of
Belloni et al. 2017
,
it can be shown that uniform covering numbers of functions in
\mathcal{G}_{3}^{0}

and
\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}
are bounded

by
\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

multiplied by some constants. Uniform covering numbers of functions
in
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{0}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{0}
,
\mathcal{G}_{5}

and
\mathcal{G}_{6}
are also bounded by
\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

multiplied by some constants. The function
\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)

is formed based on a union of functions in the sets
\mathcal{G}_{j}^{0}
,

j=1,\ldots,4
,
\mathcal{G}_{5}
and
\mathcal{G}_{6}
. Let
\Psi_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}=\left\{\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right),a\in\mathcal{A}\right\},

where
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
. Fixing

\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2}^{0}}\right|\left|Y_{a}\right|+\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1}^{0}}-\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}\left(1-g_{2d}^{0}\right)}{\left(1-g_{1d}^{0}\right)g_{2}^{0}}\right|\left|g_{3a}^{0}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|1-\frac{1\left\{D=d^{\prime}\right\}}{1-g_{1d}^{0}}\right|\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\left|g_{3a}^{0}\right|+\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\right|.

The envelop function of
f\in\Psi_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}
is defined
as
\psi{}_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}\left(W\right):=\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},v\in\left(\cup_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{0}\right)}\left|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)\right|

and we have

\displaystyle\psi{}_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}\left(W\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{3a}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{3}^{0}}\left|g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{4}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}}\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right|.

Using the facts that for
q\geq 4
,
\left\|g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\leq\left\|g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}

and
\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\leq\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi{}_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}\left(W\right)\right\|_{P,q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{3a}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{3}^{0}}\left\|g_{3a}^{0}\left(D,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{4}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}}\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}}\right)\varepsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{q}}+\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}<\infty,

and this property holds for
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Therefore,
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\psi{}_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}\left(W\right)\right\|_{P,q}<\infty

for
q\geq 4
and
f\in\Psi_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}
is uniformly bounded
and has a uniform covering entropy bounded by
\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

up to multiplication by a constant.

The case when
d=d^{\prime}

The function
\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)
is formed
by a union of functions in the sets
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{0}
,
\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}
,

\mathcal{G}_{5}
and
\mathcal{G}_{6}
. Let
\Psi_{d,d}^{0}=\left\{\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right),a\in\mathcal{A}\right\},

where
d\in\left\{0,1\right\}
. Fixing
d
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v_{a}^{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right|\left|Y_{a}\right|+\left|\frac{1\left\{D=d\right\}}{g_{1d}^{0}}\right|\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\right|+\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+1\right)\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\right|,

where
g_{4ad}^{0}=g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)
. The envelop function
of
f\in\Psi_{d,d}^{0}
is defined as
\psi{}_{d,d}^{0}\left(W\right):=\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},v\in\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{0}\cup\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}\right)}\left|\psi_{d,d,a}\left(W_{a},v\right)\right|

and we have

\psi{}_{d,d}^{0}\left(W\right)\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+1\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{4}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}}\left|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right|.

For
q\geq 4
and
\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\leq\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi{}_{d,d}^{0}\left(W\right)\right\|_{P,q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+1\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{4}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}}\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+1\right)\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A},g_{4}^{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{4}^{0}}\left\|g_{4ad}^{0}\left(D,X\right)\right\|_{P,q}\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{1}}+1\right)\varepsilon_{1}^{-\frac{1}{q}}<\infty,

and this property holds for
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Therefore,
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\psi{}_{d,d}^{0}\left(W\right)\right\|_{P,q}<\infty

for
q\geq 4
and
f\in\Psi_{d,d}^{0}
is uniformly bounded and has
a uniform covering entropy bounded by
\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

up to multiplication by a constant.

Combining the previous results, let
\Psi^{0}=\Psi_{1,1}^{0}\cup\Psi_{1,0}^{0}\cup\Psi_{0,1}^{0}\cup\Psi_{0,0}^{0}

be a union of
\Psi_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}
,
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
.
Since
\Psi^{0}
is a union of
\Psi_{d,d^{\prime}}^{0}
for
\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}
,
it is a finite union of classes of functions which are uniformly bounded
and have the uniform entropies bounded by
\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

up to multiplication by a constant. For this reason,
\Psi^{0}
has a uniform
covering number
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\epsilon,\Psi^{0},\left\|.\right\|_{Q,2}\right)\lesssim\log\left(\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0

and its envelop function

\Psi^{0}\left(W\right)=\sup_{\left(d,d^{\prime}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2},a\in\mathcal{A},v\in\cup_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{0}}\left|\psi_{d,d^{\prime},a}\left(W_{a},v\right)\right|

is also bounded. Furthermore, the uniform covering integral of
\Psi^{0}

satisfies:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\epsilon,\Psi^{0},\left\|.\right\|_{Q,2}\right)}d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{1-\log\epsilon}d\epsilon\leq\sqrt{C}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}d\epsilon<\infty

by the fact that
1-\log\epsilon\leq 1/\epsilon
for all
\epsilon>0

and
\int_{0}^{1}\epsilon^{-b}d\epsilon<\infty
for
b<1
.

The second condition in (B.1) of
Belloni et al. 2017
can also be proven
(which corresponds to Assumption A1.4). The goal of such a proof
is the same as the aim of bounding
m_{n}
in
Farbmacher et al. 2022

and
Chernozhukov et al. 2018
. But in their scenarios, they need to consider

Y,

E\left[Y|d,M,X\right]
and
E\left[E\left[Y|d,M,X\right]|d^{\prime},X\right]
,
which requires additional moment conditions, say
\left\|Y\right\|_{P,q}
,

\left\|E\left[Y|d,M,X\right]\right\|_{P,q}
and
\left\|E\left[E\left[Y|d,M,X\right]|d^{\prime},X\right]\right\|_{P,q}
.
Our scenario is less challenging since the functions
g_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)

(a c.d.f.) and
g_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)
(an expectation
of a c.d.f.) are bounded.

Verifying Assumption A.1.7

The case when
d\neq d^{\prime}

We define the following sets of functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}\left(d\right)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}x\longmapsto h_{1}\left(f\left(x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{1}\\
\left\|h_{1}\left(f\left(X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\\
\left\|h_{1}\left(f\left(X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right)-g_{1d}^{0}\left(X\right)\right\|_{P,\infty}\lesssim C\end{array}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left(m,x\right)\longmapsto h_{2}\left(f\left(m,x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{2}\\
\left\|h_{2}\left(f\left(M,X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\\
\left\|h_{2}\left(f\left(M,X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right)-g_{2d}^{0}\left(M,X\right)\right\|_{P,\infty}\lesssim C\end{array}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}\left(d\right)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left(d,m,x\right)\longmapsto h_{3}\left(f\left(d,m,x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{3}\\
\left\|h_{3}\left(f\left(d,M,X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\\
\left\|h_{3}\left(f\left(d,M,X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right)-g_{3a}^{0}\left(d,M,X\right)\right\|_{P,\infty}\lesssim C\end{array}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}\left(d^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left(d^{\prime},x\right)\longmapsto h_{4}\left(f\left(d^{\prime},x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{4}\\
\left\|h_{4}\left(f\left(d^{\prime},x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right\|_{P,2}\lesssim\delta_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\\
\left\|h_{4}\left(f\left(d^{\prime},X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right)-g_{4ad}^{0}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\right\|_{P,\infty}\lesssim C\end{array}\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,4
are vectors of coefficients
on different sets of conditioning variables and
\left\|\right\|_{0}
denotes
the
l^{0}
norm. By Assumption 2.3b,
\text{dim}\left(f\left(x\right)\right)=p\times 1
,

\text{dim}\left(f\left(m,x\right)\right)=\left(p+1\right)\times 1
,

\text{dim}\left(f\left(d,m,x\right)\right)=\left(p+2\right)\times 1
,
and
\text{dim}\left(f\left(d^{\prime},x\right)\right)=\left(p+1\right)\times 1
.
From Assumption 2.4 follows that with probability
P
no less than
1-\Delta_{n}
,

1-\hat{g}_{1d}\left(X\right)\in 1-\mathcal{G}_{1}\left(d\right)
,

\hat{g}_{2d}\left(M,X\right)\in\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)
,
\left(1-\hat{g}_{2d}\left(M,X\right)\right)\in 1-\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)
.

\hat{g}_{3a}\left(d,M,X\right)\in\mathcal{G}_{3}\left(d\right)

and
\hat{g}_{4ad}\left(d^{\prime},X\right)\in\mathcal{G}_{4}\left(d^{\prime}\right)
.
Notice that the union
\left(1-\mathcal{G}_{1}\left(d\right)\right)\cup\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)\cup\left(1-\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)\right)\cup\mathcal{G}_{3}\left(d\right)\cup\mathcal{G}_{4}\left(d^{\prime}\right)

forms the set
\mathcal{G}_{an}
. We consider the following sets of functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\{x\longmapsto h_{1}\left(f\left(x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{1},h_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{*}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(m,x\right)\longmapsto h_{2}\left(f\left(m,x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{2},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}^{*}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{3}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(d,m,x\right)\longmapsto h_{3}\left(f\left(d,m,x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{3}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{3},h_{3}\in\mathcal{H}_{3}^{*}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{4}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(d^{\prime},x\right)\longmapsto h_{4}\left(f\left(d^{\prime},x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right):\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}\right\|_{0}\leq s_{4},h_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{4}^{*}\right\},

where
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{*}
,
i=1,\ldots,4
are sets containing a finite number of monotonically increasing, continuously differentiable
link functions, possibly bounded within a certain interval (say
\left(0,1\right)
).
For example,
Belloni et al. 2017
chose some commonly used
link functions:
\left\{\mathbf{I}\text{d},\varLambda,1-\varLambda,\Phi,1-\Phi\right\}
,
where
\mathbf{I}\text{d}
is the identity function,
\varLambda

is the logistic link, and
\Phi
is the probit link. In our case,
each
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{*}
is also a subset of
\left\{\mathbf{I}\text{d},\varLambda,1-\varLambda,\Phi,1-\Phi\right\}
.
Obviously,
1-\mathcal{G}_{1}\left(d\right)\subseteq 1-\mathcal{H}_{1}
,

\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{2}
,
1-\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)\subseteq 1-\mathcal{H}_{2}

\mathcal{G}_{3}\left(d\right)\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{3}
, and
\mathcal{G}_{4}\left(d^{\prime}\right)\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{4}

by Assumption 2.3a. For functions in
1-\mathcal{G}_{1}\left(d\right)

\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)
,
1-\mathcal{G}_{2}\left(d\right)

and
\mathcal{G}_{3}\left(d\right)
, their envelope functions are
constant and bounded. As shown in
Belloni et al. 2017
, for the set
1-\mathcal{H}_{1}
,
f\left(x\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}

is VC-subgraph function with VC dimension bounded by some constant
(
s_{1}
), and
1-\mathcal{H}_{1}
is a union of at most
\binom{p}{s_{1}}

of such functions. Therefore,

\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{1},\left\|.\right\|_{Q}\right)\lesssim\left(s_{1}\log p+s_{1}\log\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0.

Using a similar argument, we obtain

\displaystyle\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{2}\cup(1-\mathcal{G}_{2}),\left\|.\right\|_{Q}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(s_{2}\log\left(p+1\right)+s_{2}\log\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0,

\displaystyle\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{3},\left\|.\right\|_{Q}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(s_{3}\log\left(p+2\right)+s_{3}\log\text{e}/\epsilon\right)\vee 0.

Concerning the functions in
\mathcal{G}_{4}\left(d\right)
, they have an
additive linear form
f\left(d^{\prime},X\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}=aD+X^{\top}\mathbf{b}
,

\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}=\left(a,\mathbf{b}\right)^{\top}
. Its envelope
function is bounded by
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{4}
being bounded and Assumption
2.3e, implying that
\left\|X\right\|_{P,q}
is bounded. Concerning the set
